***C.3. Evaluating Learning (Please see the Learning Activity Sheet for learners instructions on this activity).*** **Activity C.3: Assessing Learning Outcomes**

Facilitate the activity in the LAS. To enhance learner engagement, you may use any of the suggested strategies.

Strategy 1. Individual Performance Assessment Strategy *Instructions:*

1. Distribute the assessment sheet to all learners.
2. Instruct learners to answer the classification, graphing, and application tasks independently.
3. Remind learners to show complete solutions and label all necessary graph features.
4. Monitor learners while they work and provide clarification on instructions when necessary.
5. Collect and evaluate outputs using the provided rubric and scoring guide.
6. Discuss common errors and misconceptions after the assessment. Strategy 2: Technology-Assisted Assessment Strategy *Instructions:*
1. Allow learners to answer Part I using digital platforms such as Kahoot, Quizizz, or Google Forms.
2. Require learners to sketch the graphs in Part II using GeoGebra or Desmos and submit screenshots of their outputs.
3. Let learners solve the real-life application problems individually and submit their solutions through Google Classroom.
4. Review submitted outputs and provide feedback based on the rubric.
5. Facilitate a brief reflection session where learners discuss challenges encountered during the

Facilitate the activity in the LAS. To enhance learner engagement, you may use any of the suggested strategies. This additional exercise may be given as an assignment or homework. Remediation should be provided to learners who have not yet achieved mastery of the lesson, while enrichment activities should be offered to those who are ready for further learning.

**To evaluate learners' success in attaining the intended learning competencies, the assessment tools and strategies provided on V. the link in the table of contents can be utilized to measure understanding, skills, and application of concepts. ASSESSMENT**

**Revisit the Integrative Performance Task performed by the learners in Unit 3. Ask them to refine their outputs and present them together with outputs from the other Core subjects. VI. To assess and evaluate the effectiveness of the instruction, as well as to identify challenges and plan for improvements in this REFLECTION unit, teachers are encouraged to answer the reflective questions provided in the link indicated in the table of contents.**

##### Pag-aaral ng Kasaysayan at Lipunang Pilipino, Effective Communication, and Mabisang Komunikasyon

**IPT Title: Community Innovation Advocacy Pitch Duration: term-long**

disaster-related problem affecting the barangay while b. What real-world problems, considering its geography, culture, experiences, existing issues, or challenges will practices, and community needs. Examples may include: learners address? • Flooding

3. Application of scale interpretation through:

|  |  | • Responsible decision-making • Readiness for future studies, career goals, community involvement, and real-life situations |
|---|---|---|
| Role a. What role will the learners assume in completing the task? b. Who are they pretending to be (e.g., researcher, entrepreneur, scientist, journalist, community leader)? How does this role reflect real- life responsibilities? | : | Learners will take on the role of community innovation advocates. In this role, they will study a local community problem, design a prototype, prepare scaled models and measurements, analyze cost and feasibility, create communication materials, and present an advocacy pitch to persuade others to support, improve, adopt, scale up, or scale down the innovation. This role reflects real-life responsibilities because learners perform tasks similar to young innovators, community researchers, project designers, financial planners, communicators, and advocates. They must use evidence, collaborate effectively, and make responsible decisions to address real community needs. |
| Audience a. Who is the intended audience or beneficiary of the task? b. Who will view, use, evaluate, or be affected by the learners' output? c. How can the audience make the task more authentic and meaningful? | : | The intended audience includes: • teachers from the integrated learning areas; • invited stakeholders such as barangay officials, SK officials, community leaders, or other community representatives; and classmates. Teachers and invited stakeholders will serve as the panel of evaluators, while classmates will serve as the peer audience during the advocacy pitch. The peer audience and panel make the task more authentic and meaningful because learners must present and defend their proposed innovation before people who can evaluate its relevance, feasibility, and responsiveness to community needs and conditions. This encourages learners to support their recommendations with evidence and consider how community context, existing practices, safety, sustainability, and cost influence the design and implementation of their proposed innovation. |
| Situation a. What real-life context or scenario will make the task meaningful? b. What challenges, issues, or needs must learners respond to? c. Why is the task important or relevant in the given situation? | : | Your barangay faces disaster-related problems that affect the safety, well-being, resources, or daily lives of its residents. Examples may include flooding, storm surges, landslides, drought, water shortages, poor evacuation conditions, or other community concerns. Existing coping, preparedness, or adaptation practices of the barangay may help address these problems, but they may also have limitations. How can you, as a team of community innovation advocates, collaborate to design a feasible and sustainable innovation that addresses a disaster-related problem in the barangay, using Physics concepts, measurements, conversions, and scale, while considering its geography, culture, experiences, existing practices, and the needs of residents? To answer this question, you will investigate the problem, analyze existing practices, develop a prototype-based innovation, evaluate its |

-----

feasibility, and communicate your recommendations through an advocacy pitch.

This task allows you to apply what you have learned in different subjects to a real community concern while considering feasibility, safety, sustainability, cost, and community needs.

##### Product/ Performance

a. What output, presentation, or performance will learners create?

b. What evidence will demonstrate learners' understanding and skills?

c. How will learners integrate knowledge and skills from different learning areas?

d. What are the detailed instructions for the learners?

: Learners will deliver a Community Innovation Advocacy Pitch supported by an Advocacy Brochure. The pitch will present a disaster-related barangay problem, existing coping/adaptation practices, proposed innovation, feasibility findings, and a call to action. The brochure will serve as an audience-friendly summary of the project.

The following outputs will serve as evidence of learning:

1. Prototype Development Plan
2. Prototype Adaptability Plan
3. Feasibility and Comparison Report
4. Advocacy Brochure
5. Advocacy Pitch
6. Individual Monitoring Sheet and Work Gallery
7. Reflection Guide

Before starting the project, review Appendix A (Innovator's

##### Roadmap) to understand the project phases, sequence of tasks,

expected outputs, and learning areas integrated throughout the project.

##### LEARNER INSTRUCTIONS

1. Form your group and assign specific roles based on each

**member's strengths, interests, and skills. Roles may**

include leaders, researchers, designers, computation handlers, finance handlers, science explainers, writers, and documenters. Roles may be combined or adjusted depending on group size. Discuss and agree on each member's responsibilities to ensure meaningful participation and collaboration throughout the project. Each member must actively contribute to the project and participate in the final advocacy pitch.

2. Review Appendix A (Innovator's Roadmap) to understand the project phases, sequence of tasks, and expected outputs.
3. Accomplish the Individual Monitoring Sheet (Appendix F) throughout the project to document assigned tasks, accomplishments, means of verification, and supporting evidence. Update the sheet regularly as you complete project activities. Attach evidence in the Work Gallery, such as photos, sketches, computations, drafts, meeting notes, and short narratives.
4. Brainstorm possible disaster-related problems affecting

**your barangay. Discuss which problems are observable,**

relevant, and feasible to address through an innovation-based solution. As a group, select one problem to investigate and address throughout the project. Describe the problem by explaining who is affected, where it occurs, and why it is

-----

important to address. Record your responses in the Description of Problem subsection of Section I. Barangay Problem of the Prototype Development Plan (Appendix B).

5. Describe how the identified problem is connected to the

**barangay's geography, culture, way of life, traditions, experiences, or history. Understanding these factors can**

help you better understand the problem and design a solution that is appropriate, acceptable, and sustainable for the barangay and its residents. Record your responses in the Community Context subsection of Section I of the Prototype Development Plan (Appendix B).

6. Investigate **the existing coping, preparedness, or**

**adaptation practices used by the barangay or residents in addressing the identified problem. Gather information**

through observations, interviews, barangay documents, reports, plans, or other credible sources. Complete Section II. Existing Coping/Adaptation Practices of the Prototype Development Plan (Appendix B) by identifying the supporting evidence, describing the existing practice, and analyzing its strengths and limitations.

7. Develop a proposed innovation that addresses the

**identified barangay problem based on your analysis of the**

problem, community context, and existing coping/adaptation practices. The innovation should respond to the limitations of existing practices and be appropriate, acceptable, and sustainable for the barangay and its residents. Complete Section III. Proposed Innovation of the Prototype Development Plan (Appendix B) by describing the innovation concept, identifying the intended beneficiaries, explaining how the innovation responds to the community context identified in Section I, and discussing the science or Physics concept(s) that support its design and function.

8. Prepare a scaled sketch, blueprint, layout, or diagram of

**the proposed innovation. Clearly indicate the scale used**

and label the dimensions and measurements needed to understand the design. Attach the visual model and complete Section IV. Visual Model with Scaling of the Prototype Development Plan (Appendix B).

9. Identify the key measurements, quantities, units, and

**design specifications used in the proposed innovation.**

Complete the Key Measurements and Design Specifications table in Section IV of the Prototype Development Plan (Appendix B) by indicating the component or part, measurement or quantity, unit, and purpose or function of each item.

10. Complete the Prototype Adaptability Plan (Appendix C). Real-world solutions often need to be adjusted to meet changing community needs, resources, or environmental conditions. Using the information gathered about the barangay's geography, culture, livelihood, population, available resources, or environmental conditions, select a realistic scale-up or scale-down scenario (e.g., larger or smaller service area, increased or decreased number of users, limited budget, availability of materials, or changing environmental conditions). Revise the measurements as

-----

needed, show the required scale and conversion computations, and explain how the changes affect the design and its intended use.

11. Analyze the feasibility of the proposed innovation using

**the Feasibility and Comparison Report (Appendix D).**

Estimate the costs and expected benefits of the proposed innovation and determine whether it is practical to implement in the barangay. Consider factors such as efficiency, safety, sustainability, accessibility, available resources, and community needs. Support your analysis with appropriate computations, tables, graphs, or other evidence when needed.

12. Using the results of your investigation and feasibility

**analysis, compare the existing coping/adaptation practices and the proposed innovation. Evaluate their**

effectiveness, cost, efficiency, safety, sustainability, accessibility, and community impact. Record your findings in the Solution Comparison section of the Feasibility and Comparison Report (Appendix D) and justify whether the proposed innovation should be adopted, improved, or reconsidered.

13. Create an Advocacy Brochure using the required contents

**listed in Appendix E. Present the community problem,**

community context, existing coping/adaptation practices, proposed innovation, visual model solution comparison, Filipino paninindigan, and call to action in a clear, organized, and audience-friendly format.

14. Submit the Prototype Development Plan, Prototype

##### Adaptability Plan, Feasibility and Comparison Report, and Advocacy Brochure to the evaluation panel at least

three (3) days before the advocacy pitch.

15. Deliver a group advocacy pitch in which every member

**speaks and presents a part related to their assigned role.**

Present the identified barangay problem, community context, existing coping/adaptation practices, proposed innovation, feasibility and comparison findings, Filipino paninindigan, and call to action. Support your recommendations with evidence from your investigation and analysis. Explain how the proposed innovation addresses community needs and why it should be considered for adoption, improvement, implementation, support, or sponsorship. The pitch will be presented to a panel composed of subject teachers and invited stakeholders, such as barangay officials, SK officials, community leaders, or other relevant representatives. After the presentation, participate in a question-and-answer session and respond to the panel's questions based on your assigned role and project findings.

16. After the advocacy pitch, complete the Reflection Guide **(Appendix G).** Reflect on your role, contributions, collaboration, challenges, learning, and personal growth throughout the project. Submit the completed Reflection Guide together with the Individual Monitoring Sheet and Work Gallery (Appendix F).

Learners will have successfully completed the task when they present a community-based innovation that is relevant, feasible, evidence- based, and responsive to local needs; demonstrate accurate application of scientific, mathematical, communication, and socio- cultural concepts; collaborate effectively with others; and communicate a clear and persuasive advocacy message supported by appropriate evidence and reasoning. |
